## GHSA-mghf-wjfp-p5xm · wasmtime

2026-10-28 · RUST · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→HEADER`

### Root cause

The `FieldMap` implementation for HTTP headers did not properly enforce the single-value constraint for the `Content-Length` header. This allowed multiple `Content-Length` headers to be set, which can lead to message framing ambiguities.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially manipulate HTTP message framing, leading to request smuggling, cache poisoning, or other bypasses of security controls that rely on correct message length interpretation.

### Key diff

```
--- a/crates/wasi-http/src/field_map.rs
+++ b/crates/wasi-http/src/field_map.rs
@@ -116,6 +107,10 @@ impl FieldMap {
         }
         let (map, limit, size) = self.mutable()?;
         let key_size = header_name_size(&key);
+        // `Content-Length` may only have a single value, see `append`.
+        if key == http::header::CONTENT_LENGTH && values.len() > 1 {
+            return Err(FieldMapError::InvalidHeaderValue);
+        }
         let values = values
             .into_iter()
             .map(|v| parse_header_value(&key, v))
@@ -202,6 +197,12 @@ impl FieldMap {
             return Err(FieldMapError::Forbidden);
         }
         let value = parse_header_value(&key, value)?;
+        // Multiple `Content-Length` values make message framing ambiguous, as
+        // different parties may disagree on which one to use, so only a single
+        // value is permitted.
+        if key == http::header::CONTENT_LENGTH && self.contains_key(&key) {
+            return Err(FieldMapError::InvalidHeaderValue);
+        }
         self.append_raw(key, value)
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds explicit checks in the `append` and `set` methods of `FieldMap` to ensure that the `Content-Length` header can only have a single value. It also introduces a `remove_forbidden` helper function and enhances the `Host` body handling to filter forbidden headers and correctly apply content length limits.

## GHSA-c8p4-j97h-3vg8 · happy-dom

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The Happy DOM library, when parsing untrusted HTML, would by default load external resources referenced by `iframe` and `link stylesheet` elements. This behavior allowed an attacker to make the server-side renderer initiate requests to arbitrary URLs, including internal network resources, without explicit user configuration to disable such loading.

### Impact

An attacker could leverage this vulnerability to perform Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF), potentially accessing internal network resources, cloud metadata endpoints, or other sensitive services from the server running the Happy DOM instance.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/happy-dom/src/nodes/html-iframe-element/HTMLIFrameElement.ts
+++ b/packages/happy-dom/src/nodes/html-iframe-element/HTMLIFrameElement.ts
@@ -411,7 +411,10 @@ export default class HTMLIFrameElement extends HTMLElement {
 			return;
 		}
 
-		if (browserFrame.page.context.browser.settings.disableIframePageLoading) {
+		if (
+			browserFrame.page.context.browser.settings.disableIframePageLoading ||
+			!browserFrame.page.context.browser.settings.enableIframePageLoading
+		) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes the default behavior for loading external CSS files and iframe pages. These features are now disabled by default, requiring explicit opt-in via new `enableCSSFileLoading` and `enableIframePageLoading` configuration options. The old `disableCSSFileLoading` and `disableIframePageLoading` options are deprecated.

## GHSA-h3rc-j82p-2c3h · vllm

2026-10-28 · Python · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vLLM library used Pillow's Image.open function without restricting the allowed image formats. This allowed untrusted input, specifically EPS files, to trigger external command execution (Ghostscript) or resource exhaustion, leading to RCE or DoS.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a malicious EPS file to the vLLM service, potentially executing arbitrary commands on the host system via Ghostscript or causing the service to crash/become unresponsive due to excessive resource consumption.

### Key diff

```
--- a/vllm/multimodal/image.py
+++ b/vllm/multimodal/image.py
@@ -2,9 +2,64 @@
 # SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Copyright contributors to the vLLM project
 
 import contextlib
+from typing import Any
 
 from PIL import Image, ImageOps
 
+# Formats accepted by Image.open for untrusted multimodal media.
+# ... (truncated allowlist definition)
+
+def open_image(fp: Any) -> Image.Image:
+    """Open image bytes/files with the serving format allowlist."""
+    return Image.open(fp, formats=ALLOWED_IMAGE_FORMATS)
 
 def rescale_image_size(
     image: Image.Image, size_factor: float, transpose: int = -1
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `open_image` helper function that explicitly defines and uses an allowlist of safe image formats for Pillow's `Image.open` function. All calls to `Image.open` handling untrusted input are replaced with this new `open_image` function, preventing the processing of dangerous formats like EPS.

## GHSA-3f7j-7jwc-fxfj · systeminformation

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient sanitization of user-supplied service names (srv) when constructing PowerShell commands on Windows. Specifically, Unicode quotation marks could bypass the existing input validation, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary commands into the `wincommand` string, which was then executed via `util.powerShell`.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on the underlying Windows operating system with the privileges of the `systeminformation` process, potentially leading to full system compromise, data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/processes.js
+++ b/lib/processes.js
@@ -377,7 +377,7 @@ function services(srv, callback) {
             if (srvs[0] !== '*') {
               wincommand += ' -Filter "';
               srvs.forEach((srv) => {
-                wincommand += `Name='${srv}' or `;
+                wincommand += `Name='${srv.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9 _.\-@]/g, '')}' or `;
               });
               wincommand = `${wincommand.slice(0, -4)}"`;
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a whitelist-based sanitization for the `srv` variable before it is embedded into the PowerShell command string. It now explicitly removes any characters that are not alphanumeric, spaces, underscores, periods, hyphens, or at-signs, preventing the injection of malicious commands.

## GHSA-6757-hggm-hp2w · vllm

2026-10-28 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→OBJECT_INJECTION`

### Root cause

The system allowed shared memory (SHM) handles to be forged by an attacker. These forged handles could point to arbitrary locations in shared memory. Although insecure serialization was disabled, the ability to control the address and monotonic ID in the handle meant an attacker could trick the worker into deserializing arbitrary data from shared memory, potentially leading to object injection or other deserialization-related issues.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary object injection, potentially leading to remote code execution or other severe impacts, even with insecure serialization explicitly disabled, by manipulating the shared memory handle to point to attacker-controlled data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/vllm/distributed/device_communicators/shm_object_storage.py
+++ b/vllm/distributed/device_communicators/shm_object_storage.py
@@ -423,7 +456,8 @@ class SingleWriterShmObjectStorage:
 
     Architecture:
     - Single writer process can put(key, value) objects
-    - Multiple reader processes can get(address, monotonic_id) objects
+    - Multiple reader processes can get(address, monotonic_id, signature, key)
+      objects
     - Built on SingleWriterShmRingBuffer for efficient shared memory management
     - Thread-safe operations with reader synchronization via locks
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces HMAC-based handle signing. A random secret is stored at the beginning of the shared memory. All SHM handles (containing key, address, and monotonic_id) are now signed with this secret. Readers verify the signature before accessing shared memory, preventing forged handles from being used.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 9 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, JavaScript, Python
> → GHSA-rmj7-2vxq-3g9f
> → GHSA-j3rv-43j4-c7qm
> → GHSA-9369-69wj-7m2f
> → GHSA-337j-9hxr-rhxg
> → GHSA-w23p-wrp7-ch38
> → GHSA-hp3v-5vw7-fx9w
> → GHSA-p6vx-979v-rg4c
> → GHSA-7rv5-mrxv-xcr9

## GHSA-6jp4-8v48-cc5v · electrum

2026-10-28 · Python · **CRITICAL** 7.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The Electrum wallet did not sufficiently verify the signatures of inputs for transactions received from a malicious server. A server could replace a legitimate broadcast transaction with a different one that spends the same inputs but to a different recipient, without proper signature validation, potentially tricking the user into RBF-ing (Replace-By-Fee) the malicious transaction.

### Impact

An attacker controlling the Electrum server could replace a user's outgoing transaction with a malicious one, redirecting funds to an attacker-controlled address if the user then attempts to RBF the original transaction.

### Key diff

```
--- a/electrum/address_synchronizer.py
+++ b/electrum/address_synchronizer.py
@@ -308,6 +320,14 @@ def add_transaction(self, tx: Transaction, *, allow_unrelated=False, is_new=True
                 is_for_me = any([self.is_mine(txo.address) for txo in tx.outputs()])
                 if not is_mine and not is_for_me:
                     raise UnrelatedTransactionException()
+            # check signatures of inputs spending our coins
+            stored_tx = self.db.get_transaction(tx_hash)
+            is_new_witness = stored_tx is None or isinstance(stored_tx, PartialTransaction)
+            checked_tx = tx if is_new_witness else stored_tx  # db keeps a complete tx
+            if not self._are_new_inputs_valid(checked_tx, range(len(tx.inputs())), is_new_witness=is_new_witness):
+                if not is_new_witness:
+                    self.remove_transaction(tx_hash)
+                raise InvalidTxSignature()
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `verify_txins` callback to the `AddressSynchronizer` which is used to validate transaction input signatures. It adds checks in `add_transaction` and `_are_new_inputs_valid` to ensure that new transactions or transactions spending previously unspent outputs have valid signatures, preventing malicious server-provided transactions from being accepted.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 70 occurrences**

## GHSA-97r2-qh9m-474h · seroval

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The deserializer trusted the 'length' property of incoming JSON nodes without validating that the associated data was actually an array. An attacker could provide a non-array object with a very large 'length' value (e.g., 1e9 or 'Infinity'), causing the deserializer to allocate massive amounts of memory or iterate an unbounded number of times without corresponding data.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger an unauthenticated event-loop DoS, leading to resource exhaustion (memory or CPU) and making the application unresponsive or crashing it, with a very small malicious payload.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/seroval/src/core/context/deserializer.ts
+++ b/packages/seroval/src/core/context/deserializer.ts
@@ -276,12 +276,27 @@ function deserializeReference(
   );
 }
 
+// A node's element list arrives from untrusted input. Without this check a
+// non-array such as `{"length":1e9}` (or `{"length":"Infinity"}`) is accepted:
+// its `length` drives allocation and iteration with no matching data on the
+// wire, so a tiny payload forces unbounded work. A genuine list can only be as
+// large as the elements actually present in the input.
+function validateNodeList(
+  node: SerovalNode | SerovalObjectRecordNode,
+  list: unknown,
+): void {
+  if (!Array.isArray(list)) {
+    throw new SerovalMalformedNodeError(node as SerovalNode);
+  }
+}
+
 function deserializeArray(
   ctx: DeserializerContext,
   depth: number,
   node: SerovalArrayNode,
 ): unknown[] {
   const items = node.a;
+  validateNodeList(node, items);
   const len = items.length;
   const result: unknown[] = assignIndexedValue(
     ctx,
```

### Fix summary

A new `validateNodeList` function was introduced to explicitly check if the 'list' property of a node is an actual array using `Array.isArray()`. This validation is now applied to all relevant deserialization functions (e.g., `deserializeArray`, `deserializeProperties`, `deserializeSet`, `deserializeMap`, `deserializeStreamConstructor`, `deserializeSequence`) to ensure that array-like structures are indeed arrays before their 'length' property is used.

## GHSA-gg6h-894w-4m9c · re2js

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an inefficient handling of lookbehind regular expression matching. Specifically, lookbehind automata were restarted from the beginning of the input string for every search operation, even when the main pattern search started mid-string. This led to redundant processing and a quadratic time complexity in relation to the input length for repeated searches.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious regular expression and input string that would cause the `re2js` engine to consume excessive CPU resources, leading to a denial of service for applications using the library.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Machine.js
+++ b/src/Machine.js
@@ -131,10 +170,23 @@ class Machine {
     this.matched = false
     this.matchcap.fill(-1)
 
-    // Lookbehinds must scan from the beginning of the string to build their state table,
-    // even if the main pattern search is requested to start mid-string.
-    let currentPos = this.prog.numLb > 0 ? 0 : pos
-    let matchStartPos = pos
+    const hasLookbehinds = this.prog.numLb > 0
+    // The main pattern is only spawned once the scan reaches the requested start position.
+    const matchStartPos = pos
+    // Lookbehind automata must have observed every character before matchStartPos. Resume them
+    // from the saved state when it is not past the requested start, otherwise start them at the
+    // beginning of the input.
+    let currentPos = pos
+    let resumeLookbehinds = false
+    if (hasLookbehinds) {
+      if (lbState !== null && lbState.pos >= 0 && lbState.pos <= pos) {
+        currentPos = lbState.pos
+        resumeLookbehinds = true
+      } else {
+        currentPos = 0
+      }
+    }
+    let saveLookbehinds = hasLookbehinds && lbState !== null
 
     let runq = this.q0
     let nextq = this.q1
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `LookbehindState` class to persist the state of lookbehind automata between successive searches over the same input. This allows lookbehinds to resume from a saved position instead of restarting from the beginning, optimizing the matching process and preventing quadratic time complexity. It also refines the conditions for stopping the search and applying prefix acceleration.

## GHSA-7rv5-mrxv-xcr9 · seroval

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.3 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→OBJECT_INJECTION`

### Root cause

The `seroval` library's deserialization process for `Sequence` and `Stream` objects relied on checking for a specific property (`__SEROVAL_SEQUENCE__` or `__SEROVAL_STREAM__`) on a plain JavaScript object. An attacker could craft a malicious serialized payload that, when deserialized, would create a plain object with these properties, allowing them to bypass type checks and inject arbitrary callables or objects into the application's runtime.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution or cause denial of service by injecting malicious objects with attacker-controlled methods that would be invoked during subsequent operations, such as event listeners or iterator methods.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/seroval/src/core/sequence.ts
+++ b/packages/seroval/src/core/sequence.ts
-export interface Sequence {
-  __SEROVAL_SEQUENCE__: true;
+export class Sequence {
   v: unknown[];
   t: number;
   d: number;
-export function isSequence(value: object): value is Sequence {
-  return '__SEROVAL_SEQUENCE__' in value;
+export function isSequence(value: object): value is Sequence {
+  return value instanceof Sequence;
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes the `Sequence` and `Stream` types from plain objects with a special property to actual JavaScript classes. The `isSequence` and `isStream` functions now perform an `instanceof` check instead of a property check. This prevents attackers from forging these 'special' objects through deserialization of untrusted input, as `instanceof` cannot be bypassed by simply adding properties to a plain object.

## GHSA-83rm-6c8r-g728 · @intlify/shared

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.2 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The `escapeParameter` function, intended to sanitize HTML in translation messages, did not account for the `srcdoc` attribute. This attribute can contain an entire HTML document, which is then parsed and rendered by the browser, bypassing existing sanitization for event handlers or other attributes.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious HTML and JavaScript code into a web page by crafting a translation message containing a `srcdoc` attribute, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS). This could allow session hijacking, defacement, or redirection.

### Key diff

```
+const srcdocPattern = /(?:^|[\s"'<>/])srcdoc\s*=/i
+const srcdocAttributePattern = /(^|[\s"'<>/])s(rcdoc\s*=)/gi
...
+  if (srcdocPattern.test(html)) {
+    html = html.replace(srcdocAttributePattern, '$1&#115;$2')
+  }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces new regular expressions to detect `srcdoc` attributes. When detected, the 's' in 'srcdoc' is neutralized by replacing it with its HTML character reference (&#115;), preventing the browser from recognizing it as a valid `srcdoc` attribute.

## GHSA-434m-5hcj-pxf3 · @graphql-tools/executor-legacy-ws

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The `executorConnectionParams` variable was shared globally across all WebSocket connections and was not reset between requests. This meant that connection parameters, potentially containing sensitive authentication tokens or other user-specific data from one request, would persist and be included in the WebSocket authentication handshake for subsequent, unrelated requests from other users.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially gain unauthorized access to other users' WebSocket connections by inheriting their authentication parameters, leading to information disclosure or unauthorized actions.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/executors/legacy-ws/src/index.ts
+++ b/packages/executors/legacy-ws/src/index.ts
-  let executorConnectionParams = {};
-  let websocket: WebSocket | null = null;
+  const disposers = new Set<() => void>();
+  let operationSeq = 0;
 
-  const ensureWebsocket = (errorHandler: (error: Error) => void = err => console.error(err)) => {
-    if (websocket == null || websocket.readyState !== WebSocket.OPEN) {
-      websocket = new WebSocketImpl(subscriptionsEndpoint, 'graphql-ws', {
-        followRedirects: true,
-        headers: options?.headers,
-        rejectUnauthorized: options?.rejectUnauthorized ?? true,
-        skipUTF8Validation: true,
-      });
-
-      websocket.onopen = () => {
-        let payload: any = {};
-        switch (typeof options?.connectionParams) {
-          case 'function':
-            payload = options?.connectionParams();
-            break;
-          case 'object':
-            payload = options?.connectionParams;
-            break;
-        }
-        payload = Object.assign(payload, executorConnectionParams);
-        websocket!.send(
-          JSON.stringify({
-            type: LEGACY_WS.CONNECTION_INIT,
-            payload,
-          }),
-          (error: any) => {
-            if (error) {
-              errorHandler(error);
-            }
-          },
-        );
-      };
-
-      websocket.onerror = event => {
-        errorHandler(event.error);
-      };
-
-      websocket.onclose = () => {
-        websocket = null;
-      };
-    }
-  };
-
-  const cleanupWebsocket = () => {
-    if (websocket != null) {
-      websocket.send(
-        JSON.stringify({
-          type: LEGACY_WS.CONNECTION_TERMINATE,
-        }),
-      );
-      websocket.terminate();
-      websocket = null;
-    }
-  };
 
   const executor: DisposableExecutor = function legacyExecutor(request: ExecutionRequest) {
-    // additional connection params can be supplied through the "connectionParams" field in extensions.
-    // TODO: connection params only from the FIRST operation in lazy mode will be used (detect connectionParams changes and reconnect, too implicit?)
-    if (
-      request.extensions?.['connectionParams'] &&
-      typeof request.extensions?.['connectionParams'] === 'object'
-    ) {
-      executorConnectionParams = Object.assign(
-        executorConnectionParams, 
-        request.extensions['connectionParams'],
-      );
-    }
+    // Bound to this execution only. A later request must not inherit keys it omits.
+    const connectionParams = {
+      ...baseConnectionParams(),
+      ...requestConnectionParams(request),
+    };
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the WebSocket executor to create a new WebSocket instance and resolve connection parameters for each individual request. This ensures that connection parameters are isolated per request and do not leak between different users or operations.

## GHSA-5793-x2q3-c3q7 · highcharts

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The Highcharts library did not properly sanitize the `credits.href` option before using it to set `window.location.href`. This allowed an attacker to inject a `javascript:` URL, which would execute arbitrary JavaScript code when a user clicked on the credits link.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary JavaScript code in the context of the user's browser, leading to session hijacking, data theft, or defacement of the web page.

### Key diff

```
--- a/ts/Core/Chart/Chart.ts
+++ b/ts/Core/Chart/Chart.ts
@@ -3149,8 +3149,13 @@ class Chart {
 
         if (creds.enabled && !this.credits) {
 
+            const href = creds.href ?
+                AST.filterUserAttributes({ href: creds.href }).href :
+                void 0;
+
             /**
              * The chart's credits label. The label has an `update` method that
              * allows setting new options as per the
@@ -3171,8 +3178,8 @@ class Chart {
                         'creditsClick',
                         e as Event,
                         (): void => {
-                            if (creds.href) {
-                                win.location.href = creds.href;
+                            if (href) {
+                                win.location.href = href;
                             }
                         }
                     );
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a sanitization step for the `credits.href` value using `AST.filterUserAttributes`. This function filters out URLs that do not start with an allowed reference, effectively preventing `javascript:` URLs from being executed when the credits link is clicked.

## GHSA-58g7-7f52-h92c · @highcharts/dashboards

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application was using `innerHTML` to set the text content of a button element. This allowed any HTML tags or scripts present in the `lang.exitFullscreen` or `lang.viewFullscreen` variables to be parsed and executed by the browser.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious scripts into the page, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) attacks. This could allow them to steal user sessions, deface the website, or redirect users to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
-            button.innerElement.innerHTML =
+            button.innerElement.textContent =
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes the assignment from `innerHTML` to `textContent`. This ensures that any content assigned to the button's inner element is treated as plain text, preventing the browser from parsing it as HTML and executing any embedded scripts.

## GHSA-5ghw-9mgr-jvx2 · @highcharts/dashboards

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application directly used `setAttribute` to apply user-provided attributes to HTML elements within cell renderers. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary attributes, including event handlers like `onerror` or `onload`, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS).

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious scripts into the web page, leading to client-side attacks such as session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
-            Object.entries(options.attributes).forEach(([key, value]): void => {
-                input.setAttribute(key, value);
-            });
+            setUserAttributes(input, options.attributes);
```

### Fix summary

A new utility function, `setUserAttributes`, was introduced to sanitize user-provided attributes. This function filters attributes against a whitelist of safe form control attributes and then passes the remaining attributes through an existing AST sanitizer, preventing the injection of dangerous attributes.

## GHSA-65hg-cf3f-5m39 · highcharts

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the Highcharts library, when parsing HTML markup, would fall back to directly assigning potentially untrusted user-controlled input to `innerHTML` of a live document's `body` element. This occurred specifically when `DOMParser` failed, for example, during printing due to Chromium issues with Trusted Types.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious scripts or HTML content into the Highcharts rendering, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS). This could allow for session hijacking, defacement, or arbitrary client-side code execution.

### Key diff

```
-            const body = createElement('div');
-            body.innerHTML = markup;
-            doc = { body };
+            doc = H.doc.implementation.createHTMLDocument('');
+            doc.body.innerHTML = markup;
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the fallback mechanism for HTML parsing. Instead of assigning untrusted markup to a live document's `innerHTML`, it now creates a detached, inert HTML document using `H.doc.implementation.createHTMLDocument('')` and assigns the markup to its `body.innerHTML`. This prevents the malicious content from executing in the context of the main document.

## GHSA-6hgp-fmhr-mrg4 · @highcharts/grid-pro

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application directly set the 'href' attribute of an anchor element using user-provided input without proper sanitization. This allowed 'javascript:' URLs to be embedded, which would execute arbitrary JavaScript when the link was clicked.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious JavaScript code into the credits link. When a user clicks this link, the script would execute in the user's browser, potentially leading to session hijacking, data theft, or defacement.

### Key diff

```
--- a/ts/Grid/Core/Credits.ts
+++ b/ts/Grid/Core/Credits.ts
@@ -142,7 +143,7 @@ class Credits {
             setHTMLContent(this.textElement, text);
         }
         if (href) {
-            this.textElement.setAttribute('href', href || '');
+            this.setHref(href);
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `setHref` method that filters the `href` attribute using an AST (Abstract Syntax Tree) sanitizer. This ensures that only safe URLs are set, and 'javascript:' URLs are removed, preventing script execution.

## GHSA-gfvx-2c2g-38wq · highcharts

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the application directly used user-controlled keys from data points to create new columns or assign properties to existing objects without proper validation. Specifically, it did not filter out dangerous keys like '__proto__' or 'constructor', allowing an attacker to modify the Object.prototype.

### Impact

An attacker could manipulate the Object.prototype, leading to the injection of arbitrary properties or methods into all JavaScript objects. This could result in denial of service, unexpected behavior, or potentially remote code execution in certain contexts.

### Key diff

```
-                for (const key of Object.keys(ptOptions)) {
+                for (const key of Object.keys(ptOptions)) {
+                    if (key === '__proto__' || key === 'constructor') {
+                        continue;
+                    }
                     columns[key] ||= new Array(dataLength);
                     columns[key][i] = (ptOptions as any)[key];
                 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces checks to explicitly filter out '__proto__' and 'constructor' keys when processing data points for series columns or when preparing point options. This prevents these sensitive keys from being used to modify Object.prototype or the Object constructor.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 42 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript
> → GHSA-fw9q-39r9-c252
> → GHSA-rv5g-f82m-qrvv

## GHSA-m665-v7pm-7grj · zip

2026-10-28 · RUST · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an inconsistency in how zip archives were parsed. An attacker could craft a zip file with differing metadata between the local file headers and the central directory headers. The parser would prioritize one set of headers for certain operations (e.g., file name, size) and another for security checks or extraction, leading to a 'zip cloaking' effect.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary file overwrite and potentially remote code execution (RCE) by manipulating the perceived file names or properties, allowing them to bypass security controls and write malicious files to unexpected locations.

### Key diff

```
- pub const fn new(reader: R) -> Self {
- Self(reader)
+ pub const fn new(reader: R) -> Self {
+ Self {
+ reader,
+ enforce_header_consistency: false,
+ }
  }

+ pub const fn enforce_header_consistency(mut self, enforce: bool) -> Self {
+ self.enforce_header_consistency = enforce;
+ self
  }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `enforce_header_consistency` option for the `ZipStreamReader`. When enabled, it validates that the local file header matches the central directory entry for each file. Discrepancies between these headers, which could indicate a 'zip cloaking' attack, will now cause the extraction to fail.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 70 occurrences**

---

## GHSA-r5wg-q8fc-p6w2 · @highcharts/grid-pro

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The Highcharts Grid component was using `innerHTML` to render pagination language options and other dynamic content. This allowed attacker-controlled input, if it contained malicious HTML or script tags, to be parsed and executed by the browser.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary client-side scripts into the web page, leading to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
--- a/ts/Grid/Core/Pagination/Pagination.ts
+++ b/ts/Grid/Core/Pagination/Pagination.ts
@@ -490,7 +490,7 @@ class Pagination {
             totalPages: totalPages
         });
 
-        this.pageInfoElement.innerHTML = pageInfoText;
+        this.pageInfoElement.textContent = pageInfoText;
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces all instances of `innerHTML` with `textContent` or `innerText` when rendering dynamic content such as page numbers, ellipsis, page size labels, and page info text. This ensures that any user-provided or dynamically generated strings are treated as plain text rather than HTML.

## GHSA-rcqf-f6h2-ccg9 · @highcharts/dashboards

2026-10-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application was using `innerHTML` to render user-controlled component labels directly into the DOM. This allowed any HTML or script tags within the `components[i].text` variable to be parsed and executed by the browser.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious scripts into the dashboard, leading to cross-site scripting (XSS) attacks. This could allow them to steal user sessions, deface the application, or redirect users to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
-            gridElement.innerHTML = components[i].text;
+            gridElement.textContent = components[i].text;
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes the way component labels are rendered from `innerHTML` to `textContent`. This ensures that any HTML tags within the label text are treated as plain text rather than being parsed as executable HTML, effectively neutralizing the XSS vulnerability.
